[03:13:35.340] Thank you.
[03:13:36.870] And so
[03:13:37.920] The model we like is, whenever any piece of data in your application may have changed, re-render everything.
[03:13:42.690] That sounds crazy and it sounds kind of annoying, but it's actually really, really nice, right?
[03:13:46.710] We're not thinking about the buddy list. We're not thinking about event A, B, C, and D over time.  We're just taking a snapshot of the data model, and then we're re-rendering everything in one path.
[03:13:54.690] So you can just fit all of that stuff in your head without thinking about the entire history of what your application's done.
[03:13:59.310] And so what React does is React re-renders everything  to a virtual DOM representation, which is fast.
[03:14:03.930] and then it dips that representation with a previous representation, which is fast,
[03:14:08.340] and then it only updates the DOM with what actually changed.
[03:14:11.010] So, for example, if you render a div with a class name, and that class name attribute changes, we have to figure out, hey, that div is still there, so don't recreate the element, just change the class name property to be what the user wanted it to be.
[03:14:23.820] And this leads to the ability to build really expressive applications. So this is some definition of expressive power, but I like this one better.
